Let's recalc: For full convolution length 2n-1, c_k count pairs i,j in A with i+j=k. There are w^2 pairs total, average over sums ≈w^2/(2n)=α^2 n/2. So max interior at least α^2 n for random, score=2n*α^2 n/(α^2 n^2)=2. To reduce max below 2, need reduce interior pairwise counts relative to total weight: i.e., arrange A so that pairs with sums in middle are fewer, while pairs with sums near edges are more. Since total pairs fixed, average is half of interior expectation. We need max interior ≤0.75 α^2 n (for score 1.5), i.e., 25% below the uniform-distribution interior mean. Let's attempt to design an optimization method that can find below 1.5 from scratch.

Observation: Objective can be minimized using "Sinkhorn-like" or "mirror descent" on the space of nonnegative sequences with fixed sum. The natural parameterization a_i = exp(θ_i)/sum exp? Nonnegative with sum fixed. Then c_k = ∑ a_i a_{k-i}. We want minimize max c_k. This is like learning a distribution p_i to make pairwise sums avoid masses. We can use multiplicative updates:

Given current p (sum1), let K be top lags with c_k near max. We want adjust θ to reduce c_k. Gradient of c_k w.r.t θ_i = p_i(2 p_{k-i} - c_k?) Actually under softmax sum constraint, gradient of c_k is p_i (p_{k-i}+p_{?} - c_k?) Let's derive unconstrained p with sum1: ∂c_k/∂p_i = 2p_{k-i} (valid) but with sum constraint, direction. Multiplicative update p_i ← p_i * exp(-η * 2 p_{k-i}) reduces c_k for critical k. For multiple critical lags weighted α_k, update p_i *= exp(-η * 2∑ α_k p_{k-i}), then renormalize. This is multiplicative weights. It tends to equalize c_k by reducing pairs for hot sums.

This is similar to "tropical" / "convex cost". Could work. We can weight lags by softmax of c to handle max. Update:
w_k = softmax(β c_k / T) maybe, sum1. Then for each i, t_i = ∑_{k} w_k * 2 p_{k-i} (where valid). Update p_i *= exp(-η t_i) / sum. Since total sum preserved after normalization. This is a natural gradient (Fisher) for minimizing ∑ w_k log? Actually it decreases weighted sum of c_k. If weights concentrate on max, minimax.

Parameters: temperature T adaptive (e.g., T = max(c) + small), learning rate η small. For n=512, can run many iterations using FFT? t_i is convolution of w (length 2n-1) with p reversed? We can compute via fast convolution. Or direct O(n^2) if n small.

Another method: Use "alternating projections" / "waterfilling". For current p, define potential c_k. We want lower max. If max c > target M, for each k with c_k close M, solve for q with p*q ≤ M maximizing sum (SOTA). Can approximate q via multiplicative updates too.

SOTA:
- Normalize f sum = sqrt(2n) so score = max_b (if no clamp? Actually evaluate =2n M/S^2. If S^2=2n, score=M. Yes.)
- M = max(f*f).
- Solve LP: maximize sum(g) subject to g>=0, (f*g) <= M (vector length 2n-1).
Note f fixed nonnegative normalized. The feasible set includes g=f (since f*f<=M) with sum sqrt(2n). The LP optimum sum* >= sqrt(2n). Let g = optimal. Rescale to sum sqrt(2n): g_rescaled. Then convex combo f_new=(1-t)f + t g_rescaled. Claim max nonincreasing? Maybe because g_rescaled may have f*g? Not directly. But iteration.

Can we solve this LP more simply? Since f nonnegative, K rows are convolutions. This is a packing LP. We can solve via dual with nonnegative y. The dual constraints: for each i=0..n-1, ∑_{k=...} f_{k-i} y_k ≥1. Let F = f padded? If we define y length 2n-1 padded with zeros to length 2n-1. Constraint (f * y)[i +?] Let's derive:

Let j=k-i: ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} f_j y_{i+j} ≥1. This is cross-correlation (f forward, y sliding). Let y padded to length n+n-1. Define y_pad length 2n-1. For i=0..n-1: d_i = ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} f_j y_{i+j}. Need d_i≥1. Minimize M sum y. This is a covering problem. We can scale out M: z = M y, minimize sum z s.t. d_i≥1. The optimum dual gives active lags.

This dual resembles finding a nonnegative measure on lags whose smoothed version covers f^? It may be solved by greedy algorithm: Choose y_k large where it covers deficient d_i. Since all coefficients f_j nonnegative. The constraint matrix is interval matrices (Toeplitz). Could solve via linear programming or even greedy due to total monotonicity? The constraints are sliding dot products with fixed nonnegative vector f. This is like placing weights on positions to cover demand 1 given kernel f. def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic()+budget_s-5
    best_seq = height_sequence_1 if valid else None
    best_score = evaluate_sequence(best_seq)
    
    # Try several lengths and strategies
    lengths = [128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048] maybe depending time.
    for n in ...:
       for trial:
          p = random_init(n, rng, strategy)
          improve with coordinate_descent(p, max_time)
          if score improve: update best
    return best_seq.tolist()
```

Need refine coordinate descent to be effective. Let's test on continuous problem: coordinate-wise minimization of max autoconvolution is known as "coordinatewise minimization of L∞ autoconvolution". It may converge to a sequence close to optimal. We can use stochastic ordering and line search.

Implementation details for speed:

Function `score_from_c(c, n, sum_a)`:
   if sum_a < 0.01: inf
   return 2*n*np.max(c)/sum_a**2

But we can compute normalized p sum1, score=2*n*np.max(c).

Coordinate update function `update_coordinate(p, c, i, grid, n)` returns new p,c,score, improved.

Pseudo:
```
def coord_minimize(p, c, i, n, n_grid=64, refine=20):
    old = p[i]
    D = 1.0 - old
    # affected slice
    ks = np.arange(i, i+n)
    j = ks - i  # 0..n-1
    c_k = c[ks]
    coeff = 2.0 * p[j]
    const = -2.0 * old * p[j]
    # middle correction at j==i if within
    mid = (j == i)
    # if mid, replace linear part with x^2; set coeff[mid]=0, const[mid]=-old*old, mididx
    if np.any(mid): coeff[mid]=0; const[mid]=-old*old
    # unaffected max
    max_unaff = 0.0
    if i>0: max_unaff = max(max_unaff, np.max(c[:i]))
    if i < n-1: # suffix starts i+n, length (2n-2)-(i+n)+1 = n-1-i
        max_unaff = max(max_unaff, np.max(c[i+n:]))
    base_score = current_score
    # candidate x list
    if D>1e-12:
       r = np.logspace(-6, 3, n_grid) # ratios up to 1000
       xs = r*D
    else:
       xs = np.logspace(-6,3,n_grid)
    # ensure old included
    xs = np.concatenate(([0.0], xs, [old]))
    # Also include old * 10**(-2,-1,1,2) maybe already
    x_candidates = unique sorted clip(0, 1e6)
    # evaluate grid
    best_x=old; best_val=base_score
    for x in xs:
       aff = c_k + coeff*x + const
       if mid_count: aff[mididx] += x*x - old*old  # but we already set const -old^2, coeff0; adding x^2
       maxc = max(max_unaff, np.max(aff))
       S = D + x
       if S < 1e-12: continue
       val = 2.0*n*maxc/(S*S)
       if val < best_val: ...
    # refine around best_x bounds
    left = 0 if best_idx==0 else xs[best_idx-1]
    right = xs[-1] if best_idx==last else xs[best_idx+1]
    for _ in range(refine): golden...
    if best_val < base_score - 1e-12:
       S = D + best_x
       p_new = p / S
       p_new[i] = best_x / S
       # update c? Could recompute convolution from p_new for accuracy (O(n^2) maybe too slow each coordinate). We can update c incrementally:
       # First scale all c by 1/S^2
       c_new = c / (S*S)
       # For affected ks: unnormalized affected value = c_k + coeff*best_x + const (+mid x^2)
       aff = c_k + coeff*best_x + const
       if mid: aff[mid]= aff[mid]+best_x*best_x # since const -old^2
       c_new[ks] = aff / (S*S)
       # Due to numerical, set nonnegative clip
       p_new = np.clip(p_new,0,None); normalize? p_new sum should 1.
       # But p_new[i] = best_x/S; p_new others = old_vals/S.
       return p_new,c_new,best_val
```

But wait: In f(x), unaffected c are original unnormalized values c_outside. After normalization, they scale by 1/S^2. Affected unnormalized uses current c_k (which includes old value) plus delta. For middle, delta = x^2 - old^2, not -old^2 + x^2? yes. For non-mid, delta=2(x-old)p[j]. But p[j] are old (unnormalized) values. This is correct for unnormalized vector q where q[i]=x, q[j]=old p[j] for j≠i. Then normalize q/S. Good.

However, when computing coeff = 2*p[j] and const=-2*old*p[j], if j=i for middle, we shouldn't use that. We set mid.

Unaffected max: for slices outside ks. Since c length 2n-1, ks = i:i+n? For i=n-1, ks index n-1 to 2n-2 length n, suffix none, prefix 0..n-2. Good.

Potential issue: If current best max is in unaffected slice and x update scales it down (S>1) or up (S<1), the objective may improve due to scaling even if affected not. Our F includes max_unaff / S^2. Good.

We can further reduce grid dimensions by searching ratio t = x/D with D=0 special. Since F depends on D scale? If D>0, define t=x/D. Then evaluate with max_unaff/D^2 etc. But current code fine.

We need manage time and memory. If n large, grid + refine per coordinate heavy. We can reduce n_grid=40, refine=12. Use random coordinate order. Stop coordinate cycle if no improvement.

But if we recompute c from p after every coordinate, O(n^2) cost maybe high. Incremental update O(n) and p scaling O(n). However after many updates, numerical errors can accumulate. We can periodically recompute c = np.convolve(p,p) and renormalize p to sum1 (e.g., every 10 coordinates or after each cycle). Use `np.convolve` for n<=1024 O(n^2) ~1M, okay every cycle. For n=2048 ~4M, okay every few cycles.

Actually incremental c update with scaling all c by 1/S^2 and replacing affected c works exactly in exact arithmetic. But p scaling all coordinates; subsequent coeff uses updated p. Good. Floating errors minor.

We must ensure c length 2n-1. For n=1024, c=2047.

C loop maybe 0.05s? Python overhead 51k * maybe 5us? 0.25s. Full cycle maybe 1s. 1800s -> 1800 cycles? Maybe too optimistic but okay. For n=2048, 4x -> maybe 4s per cycle -> 450 cycles. Can generalize to multiple segments with fractions to optimize peak. Suppose divide into M segments with constant sums H_m, each length length_m proportional? def _eval_x(x, c_k, coeff, const, mid_pos, old, max_unaff, n):
   aff = c_k + coeff*x + const
   if mid_pos is not None:
       aff[mid_pos] = aff[mid_pos] + x*x  # since const includes -old^2, coeff 0
   maxc = max(max_unaff, np.max(aff))
   S = 1 - old + x
   return 2.0*n*maxc/(S*S)

Acceptance and update:
If best_val < score - 1e-12:
   S = 1-old+best_x
   if S <=0: skip (x=old? S=1)
   p_new = p / S
   p_new[i] = best_x / S
   # c update
   c_new = c / (S*S)
   aff = c_k + coeff*best_x + const
   aff[mid_pos] += best_x*best_x
   c_new[ks] = aff/(S*S)
   # Due to roundoff, normalize p and recompute score maybe
   p_new = np.clip(p_new,0,None)
   ssum=p_new.sum()
   if ssum <=0: skip
   p_new /= ssum
   # If we renormalize after clipping, c scaling may be off; better recompute c if we renormalize.
   # Clipping should not change (p/S nonnegative, sum=1 within float). To be safe, don't renormalize if sum close; else recompute c.
   if abs(ssum-1)>1e-9: p_new/=ssum; c_new=np.convolve(p_new,p_new)
   else: c_new = c_new # already
   # Ensure nonnegative c
   np.clip(c_new,0,None,out=c_new)
   score_new = 2*n*np.max(c_new)
   if score_new < score - 1e-12: return p_new,c_new,score_new,True
   else: # fallback recompute and accept if better using direct
       c_check = np.convolve(p_new,p_new)
       score_check = 2*n*np.max(c_check)
       if score_check < score -1e-12: p_new/=sum...; return
       else: updated False
```

Coordinate loop:
```
def optimize_coordinate(p, c, score, n, max_time, rng, grid_count=48, refine_count=24, order='random'):
   start = time.monotonic()
   while time.monotonic() < max_time:
       order = rng.permutation(n)
       improved=False
       for i in order:
          if time left < 0.5: break
          p,c,score,imp = coordinate_update(...)
          if imp: improved=True
       if not improved: break
       # recompute exact every cycle? maybe every 5 cycles always
       p_sum=p.sum(); if abs>1e-8: p/=p_sum
       c=np.convolve(p,p); score=2n maxc
   return p,c,score
```

Let's outline linprog:
- p: length n, sum1. M = np.max(p*p conv).
- Constraints: K @ g <= M * ones(2n-1), g >=0.
- Objective: maximize sum g => minimize -1^T g.
- Build sparse K. Rows k=0..2n-2. For i=0..n-1, j=k-i in 0..n-1, K[k,i]=p[j]. This is banded. Use scipy.sparse.coo_matrix.
- linprog(c=-ones, A_ub=K, b_ub=M*ones, bounds=(0,None), method='highs'). For n=128, constraints255, nz~8k; easy. n=512, constraints1023,nz~131k; maybe okay. n=1024 ~1M; maybe heavier but okay maybe.
- If optimal g has sum >1 (should), scale to sum1. New score computed. Convex combo: q = (1-t)p + t g_scaled. Choose t e.g. 0.5 or line search for t in [0,1] minimizing score. Since both p and g_scaled sum1. We can do scalar minimize over t (0..1) using grid/golden; convolution at t is quadratic: c(t) bilinear? Convolution of (1-t)p+t g: c(t)= (1-t)^2 c_p + 2t(1-t) cross + t^2 c_g. We can compute cross once via np.convolve(p,g). Then score(t) =2n max_k c_k(t). We can find best t by grid 0,0.05,...1 and refine. This can improve quickly.
- Iterate a few times.

Let's implement coordinate update with local variables and avoid function call per x? We can define a helper `_eval_x_values(xs)` vectorized over grid? To reduce Python overhead, we can evaluate all grid xs at once if matrix size manageable:

Coordinate update function with vectorized grid:
```
def _coordinate_update(p,c,score,i,grid_log_range,grid_count):
   n=p.size; old=p[i]; D=1-old
   scale=max(D,1e-6)
   xs = np.unique(np.concatenate(([0.0, old], scale*np.logspace(*log_range, grid_count))))
   xs = xs[xs>=0]
   # maybe cap xs <= 1e6
   c_k = c[i:i+n]
   coeff = 2.0 * p
   const = -2.0 * old * p
   mid = i
   coeff[mid]=0.0; const[mid]=-old*old
   max_unaff... 
   aff = c_k + coeff[None,:]*xs[:,None] + const[None,:]
   aff[:,mid] += xs*xs
   max_aff = np.max(aff, axis=1)
   maxc = np.maximum(max_unaff, max_aff)
   S = D + xs
   vals = 2*n*maxc/(S*S)
   # handle S=0 (if D=0 and x=0) set inf
   idx = np.nanargmin(vals)
   best_x=xs[idx]; best_val=vals[idx]
   if best_val >= score: return no
   # refine around best: create local log grid from max(argsort?)
   # bbox left/right adjacent or expand
   left = 0.0 if idx==0 else xs[idx-1]
   right = xs[-1] if idx==len-1 else xs[idx+1]
   # ensure width
   mid_x = best_x if best_x>0 else max(left,right,1e-9)
   local = mid_x * 10**np.linspace(-1,1,refine_count)
   local = np.unique(np.concatenate((local,[left,best_x,right])))
   # evaluate local similarly
   ...
   if improved:
       S=...
       c_new = c/(S*S)
       aff_best = c_k + coeff*best_x + const
       aff_best[mid] += best_x*best_x
       c_new[i:i+n]=aff_best/(S*S)
       p_new = p/S; p_new[i]=best_x/S
       # Maybe renormalize if sum off
       return p_new,c_new,best_val
```

How to expand a prototype p_R (sum1) to length 3809:
- Let R = len(p_R). Need block lengths L_i positive integers sum 3809. Use base L = 3809 // R, distribute remainder.
- For each prototype value h_i, block of length L_i with constant value h_i / L_i. Then each block sum = h_i, total sum=1. Concatenate. This is one zoom. As L_i large, score approaches score(p_R). If R e.g. 64, block lengths ~59, good. If R=8, block lengths ~476, very good.
- The finite block score may be slightly above prototype due to intra-block transitions? Actually the exact sequence is piecewise constant; its convolution is a piecewise-smooth approximation to the prototype convolution with resolution L. The maximum may be close, maybe slightly higher by O(1/L). For R=8,L~476, very close. So if prototype score <1.48, final <1.5027.
- We can then run coordinate descent on the full length 3809 for some time to reduce finite effects and maybe improve score further. Starting from expanded structure, coordinate descent can adjust values within blocks while preserving macro pattern. This is powerful.

Thus algorithm:
1. Search for good prototype sequences of moderate length R (e.g., 4..256) via coordinate/random; keep best prototype by its internal score (sum1 length R).
2. Expand best prototype to length 3809 (or maybe 2000/4000) and evaluate; keep if good.
3. Run coordinate descent on the expanded full-length sequence for remaining time (with smaller grid to fit) to polish. Also directly coordinate on some full-length random/expanded starts.

This covers length constraint and leverages short optimization.

Expansion function:
```
def _expand_to_length(proto, target_len):
    R=len(proto); proto=np.asarray(proto,float)
    s=proto.sum(); if s<=0: return zeros?
    h=proto/s
    base = target_len // R
    rem = target_len % R
    lengths = [base+1 if i<rem else base for i in range(R)]
    # If base=0 (target_len<R), can't; but R<=256,target 3809 so base>=7.
    out=np.empty(target_len)
    idx=0
    for i,L in enumerate(lengths):
       out[idx:idx+L] = h[i]/L
       idx+=L
    # normalize (sum exactly1)
    out /= out.sum()
    return out
```

Coordinate optimize:
```
def _coordinate_optimize(p,c,score,max_time,rng,grid_count,refine_count):
    n=p.size
    start=time.monotonic()
    stagnant=0
    while time.monotonic() < max_time - 1.0:
       order = rng.permutation(n)
       improved=False
       for i in order:
          if time.monotonic() > max_time - 0.2: break
          p,c,score,imp = _coordinate_update(...)
          if imp:
             improved=True
       # recompute exact
       s=p.sum()
       if s<=0: break
       p = p / s
       c = np.convolve(p,p)
       score = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c))
       if not improved:
          stagnant +=1
          if stagnant >=2: break
       else: stagnant=0
    return p,c,score
```
But `max_time` is allotted, not deadline? Use `allowed_until` maybe. We can pass `deadline` directly and loop until `time.monotonic() < deadline`. Simpler:
```
def _coordinate_optimize_until(p,c,score,deadline,rng,...):
   while time.monotonic() < deadline: ...
```
For each trial, compute `trial_deadline = min(global_deadline, time.monotonic()+slice)`.

We must also stop if score reaches near lower bound? lower=2n/(2n-1); if score < lower+1e-9, stop (can't improve). But lower ~1, target 1.5; not needed.

   3: dirichlet alpha=0.2

   6: paired extreme
   7: two_level
   8: binary+noise
   9: monotone increasing + noise

   11: random sorted? etc.
   normalize, if sum<1e-6 fallback uniform
```

LP function:
```
def _lp_polish(p, c, score, deadline, max_iters=20):
   try: from scipy.optimize import linprog; from scipy import sparse
   except: return p,c,score
   n=p.size
   if n > 512: return # too heavy
   p = np.asarray(p,float).copy(); p/=sum
   M = np.max(c)
   K = ...
   for it in range(max_iters):
      if time> deadline-30: break
      obj = -np.ones(n)
      b = np.full(2*n-1, M)
      res = linprog(c=obj, A_ub=K, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
      if not res.success or res.x is None: break
      g = np.asarray(res.x,float); np.clip(g,0,None)
      gs = g.sum()
      if gs <=1: break
      g = g/gs
      # find best t between 0 and1
      cg = np.convolve(g,g); cross=np.convolve(p,g)
      def val_t_array(ts): c_t = (1-ts)^2 c + 2ts(1-ts) cross + ts^2 cg; score=2n max
      ts=np.linspace(0,1,101); choose best; refine golden maybe
      if score_t > score: t=0.5? Use best_t if improved else continue
      p = (1-t)*p + t*g
      p/=sum; c=np.convolve(p,p); score=2n max
      M = np.max(c)
   return p,c,score
```
But K built from p; if p changes, rebuild each iteration. For n=128, okay. For n=256, maybe. The tornado uses f=p; constraint p*g≤M. It may produce g. Could help.

Need build K efficiently:
```
rows=[]; cols=[]; vals=[]
for i in range(n):
    # K[k,i]=p[k-i] for k in i..i+n-1
    ks = np.arange(i, i+n)
    js = np.arange(n) # k-i
    rows.extend(ks); cols.extend([i]*n); vals.extend(p[js])
K = sparse.coo_matrix((vals,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n)).tocsr()
```
This is O(n^2) Python loops extending lists; for n=256, 65k, okay. Use numpy:
```
i_arr = np.arange(n)[:,None]  # n x1
j_arr = np.arange(n)[None,:]  # 1 x n
k_idx = (i_arr + j_arr).ravel()
i_idx = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
vals = np.tile(p, n)  # for each i, p[0..n-1]
K = sparse.csr_matrix((vals, (k_idx, i_idx)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
```

Random initial strategies for prototypes:
```
def _make_initial(n, strategy, rng):
    if strategy == 0: p = np.ones(n)
    elif 1: p = rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,1.0))
    elif 2: p = rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,0.5))
    elif 3: p = rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,0.2))
    elif 4: p = np.exp(rng.normal(0,1.0,n)); p/=p.sum()
    elif 5: p = np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.4,n)); p/=p.sum()
    elif 6: p = _gen_paired(n,rng,0.2)
    elif 7: p = _gen_paired(n,rng,1.0)
    elif 8: p = _gen_two_level(n)
    elif 9: x=np.arange(n); p=np.exp(1.0*x/n)+0.1*rng.random(n)
    elif 10: p=np.exp(-1.0*x/n)+0.1*rng.random(n)
    elif 11: p=(rng.random(n)>0.5).astype(float); p += 0.05*rng.random(n)
    else: p=rng.random(n)
    p=np.asarray(p,float); p=np.clip(p,0,None); s=p.sum(); if s>0: p/=s else uniform
    return p
```
For `_gen_two_level`: if n even, first half 2/3 total, second 1/3; if odd, distribute.
```
m=n//2
p=zeros; p[:m]= (2/3)/m; p[m:]= (1/3)/(n-m); if odd last? simpler: p[:m]=ratio; ensure sum by normalize.
```

Maybe add "paired random" with mirror extreme. Implementation:
```
def _gen_paired(n,rng,alpha_w=1.0,alpha_split=0.3):
    m=(n+1)//2
    weights = rng.dirichlet(np.full(m, alpha_w))
    p=zeros(n)
    for r in range(m):
       a=r; b=n-1-r
       if a==b:
          p[a]=weights[r]
       else:
          if rng.random()<0.75:
             if rng.random()<0.5: q=(1.0,0.0)
             else: q=(0.0,1.0)
          else:
             q=rng.dirichlet([alpha_split,alpha_split])
          p[a]=weights[r]*q[0]
          p[b]=weights[r]*q[1]
    p/=p.sum()
    return p
```
This may produce zeros; okay.

Pseudo:
```
def _coordinate_optimize_until(p,c,score,deadline,rng,grid,refine_count,max_perturb=3):
    n=p.size; best_p=p.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); best_score=score; stagnant=0; pert=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.5:
        order=np.random? rng.permutation(n)
        improved=False
        start=time.monotonic()
        for i in order:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2: break
            p,c,score,imp = _coordinate_update_within(p,c,score,i,grid,refine)
            if imp:
                improved=True
                if score < best_score - 1e-10:
                    best_p=p.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); best_score=score
        # recompute exact
        s=p.sum(); ... p,c,score
        if score < best_score - 1e-10: update best
        if improved: stagnant=0
        else:
            stagnant+=1
            if stagnant >=3 and pert < max_perturb:
                # perturb best and recompute
                p = best_p * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.25,n))
                p=np.clip(p,0,None); p/=p.sum(); c=np.convolve(p,p); score=2n max
                # If perturbation somewhat better? If worse, okay but current changed.
                p = p.copy(); pert+=1; stagnant=0
                # maybe set current to perturbed best, not best, to escape
        # If score much worse than best and improved? current could be worse due to perturbation; next coordinates may improve from there.
    return best_p,best_c,best_score
```
But if perturbation worsens, and time left, coordinates from perturbed may or may not beat best. Good. However if perturbation not improve within current cycle, the `improved` flag might be false; stagnant increments again. We set stagnant=0 after perturb, but if next cycle no coordinate improves from perturbed, it may perturb again. max_perturb limits.

Maybe better to reset current to perturbed best only if perturbation score not too bad. Fine.

Detailed code for `_coordinate_update`:

```
def _coordinate_update(p, c, score, i, grid, refine_count):
    n = p.size
    old = float(p[i])
    if old < 0: old = 0.0
    D = 1.0 - old
    if D < 0.0: D = 0.0
    ks = slice(i, i + n)
    c_k = c[ks]
    coeff = 2.0 * p.copy()
    const = -2.0 * old * p.copy()
    mid = i
    if 0 <= mid < n:
        coeff[mid] = 0.0
        const[mid] = -old * old
    # max unaffected
    max_unaff = 0.0
    if i > 0:
        m = float(np.max(c[:i]))
        if m > max_unaff: max_unaff = m
    end = i + n
    if end < n + n - 1:
        m = float(np.max(c[end:]))
        if m > max_unaff: max_unaff = m
    xs = np.empty(grid.size + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    xs[:-1] = grid
    xs[-1] = old
    # filter valid finite, >=0, <=1e4
    mask = np.isfinite(xs) & (xs >= 0.0) & (xs <= 1e4)
    xs = xs[mask]
    if xs.size == 0:
        return p,c,score,False
    aff = c_k[None, :] + coeff[None, :] * xs[:, None] + const[None, :]
    aff[:, mid] += xs * xs
    max_aff = np.max(aff, axis=1)
    maxc = max_unaff if max_unaff > 0 else 0.0 # np.maximum with scalar
    maxc = np.maximum(max_unaff, max_aff)
    S = D + xs
    vals = np.full(xs.size, np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
    valid = S > 1e-15
    vals[valid] = (2.0 * n) * maxc[valid] / (S[valid] * S[valid])
    idx = int(np.argmin(vals))
    best_x = float(xs[idx])
    best_val = float(vals[idx])
    if not np.isfinite(best_val) or best_val >= score - 1e-12 * max(1.0, score):
        return p,c,score,False
    # refine
    if refine_count > 0 and xs.size > 1:
        if idx == 0:
            left = 0.0
        else:
            left = float(xs[idx - 1])
        if idx == xs.size - 1:
            right = float(xs[idx]) * 10.0  # extend if best at max
        else:
            right = float(xs[idx + 1])
        if best_x > 0.0:
            local = best_x * np.logspace(-1.0, 1.0, refine_count)
        else:
            # use right if positive else max grid
            base_right = right if right > 0.0 else 1.0
            base_left = max(0.0, left)
            if base_right > base_left:
                local = np.logspace(np.log10(max(base_left,1e-9)), np.log10(base_right), refine_count)
            else:
                local = np.logspace(-8.0, 0.0, refine_count)
        local = np.concatenate((local, [left, best_x, right]))
        local = np.unique(local[np.isfinite(local) & (local >= 0.0) & (local <= 1e4)])
        if local.size > 0:
            aff = c_k[None, :] + coeff[None, :] * local[:, None] + const[None, :]
            aff[:, mid] += local * local
            max_aff = np.max(aff, axis=1)
            maxc = np.maximum(max_unaff, max_aff)
            S = D + local
            vals2 = np.full(local.size, np.inf, dtype=np.float64)
            valid = S > 1e-15
            vals2[valid] = (2.0 * n) * maxc[valid] / (S[valid] * S[valid])
            idx2 = int(np.argmin(vals2))
            if float(vals2[idx2]) < best_val:
                best_x = float(local[idx2]); best_val = float(vals2[idx2])
    if best_val >= score - 1e-12 * max(1.0, score):
        return p,c,score,False
    S = D + best_x
    if S <= 0: return ...
    S2 = S*S
    c_new = c / S2
    aff_best = c_k + coeff * best_x + const
    aff_best[mid] += best_x * best_x
    c_new[ks] = aff_best / S2
    p_new = p / S
    p_new[i] = best_x / S
    # normalize
    snew = float(np.sum(p_new))
    if not np.isfinite(snew) or snew <= 0.0:
        return p,c,score,False
    if abs(snew - 1.0) > 1e-9:
        p_new = p_new / snew
        c_new = c_new / (snew*snew)
    else:
        p_new = p_new / snew
        c_new = c_new / snew  # wait if snew close1, dividing c by snew? Actually c_new already corresponds to q/S. If p_new renormalized by 1/snew, c should scale by 1/snew^2. If snew=1+eps, c_new/(1+2eps). We can just do p_new/=snew; c_new/=snew*snew always.
```
Always do `p_new /= snew; c_new /= (snew*snew)` for consistency. Since snew should be 1, cost small.
```
    np.clip(p_new, 0.0, None, out=p_new)
    np.clip(c_new, 0.0, None, out=c_new)
    # Renormalize after clip?
    s2 = float(p_new.sum())
    if s2 <=0: return
    if abs(s2-1)>1e-12:
       p_new/=s2; c_new/=s2*s2
    score_new = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c_new))
    if score_new < score - 1e-12*max(1.0,score):
         return p_new,c_new,score_new,True
    else:
         # try exact recompute to be safe if discrepancy
         c_check = np.convolve(p_new,p_new)
         score_check = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c_check))
         if score_check < score - 1e-12*max(1.0,score):
             return p_new, c_check, score_check, True
         return p,c,score,False
```

LP helper details top-level no closures:
```
def _lp_polish(p, c, score, deadline, rng):
    try:
        from scipy.optimize import linprog
        from scipy import sparse
    except Exception:
        return p,c,score
    n=p.size
    if n < 2 or n > 128: return
    p = np.asarray(p,float).copy(); p = p/np.sum(p)
    c = np.convolve(p,p); score=2n max
    best_p=p.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); best_score=score
    M = float(np.max(c))
    # build K
    for it in range(4):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60: break
       i_idx = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
       j_idx = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
       k_idx = (i_idx + j_idx).ravel()? Actually i_idx and j_idx already 1D length n*n from repeat/tile. k_idx = i_idx + j_idx.
       vals = np.tile(p, n)  # for each i, values p[j]
       K = sparse.csr_matrix((vals, (k_idx, i_idx)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
       b = np.full(2*n-1, M)
       obj = -np.ones(n)
       try:
          res = linprog(c=obj, A_ub=K, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n, method='highs')
       except Exception:
          break
       if res is None or not res.success: break
       g=np.asarray(res.x,float); np.clip(g,0,None)
       gs=g.sum()
       if gs <= 1.0: break
       g/=gs
       # find t
       cg=np.convolve(g,g); cross=np.convolve(p,g)
       ts=np.linspace(0,1,51)
       best_t=0.0; best_val=score
       for t in ts:
           ct=(1-t)**2*c + 2*t*(1-t)*cross + t*t*cg
           val=2*n*float(np.max(ct))
           if val < best_val:
               best_val=val; best_t=t
       if best_t <=0 or best_val >= score -1e-10:
           # maybe g itself good? evaluate g
           valg=2n*max(cg)
           if valg < best_val: best_val=valg; best_t=1
       if best_val >= score -1e-10: break
       # refine t around best_t
       lo = max(0, best_t-0.1); hi=min(1,best_t+0.1)
       for _ in range(20):
           t_mid=0.5*(lo+hi)
           # Need scalar minimization non-unimodal? Use grid refine instead
       # simpler grid 201
       ts=np.linspace(max(0,best_t-0.2), min(1,best_t+0.2), 101)
       ... choose
       p=(1-best_t)*p + best_t*g; p/=p.sum(); c=np.convolve(p,p); score=2n max
       if score < best_score: update best
       M = np.max(c)
    return best_p,best_c,best_score
```

def _clean_array(arr):
    a=np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim!=1: a=a.ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0,out=a)
    return a

def _internal_score(p):
   n=p.size
   if n<1: return inf
   s=float(np.sum(p))
   if s<=0: return inf
   q=p/s
   c=np.convolve(q,q)
   return float(2.0*n*np.max(c))

def _score_with_conv(p,c): return float(2.0*p.size*max(0,np.max(c)))

Let's write `_lp_polish` carefully.

```
def _lp_polish(p,c,score,deadline,max_iters=2):
    try:
       from scipy.optimize import linprog
       from scipy import sparse
    except Exception:
       return p,c,score
    n=int(p.size)
    if n<2 or n>96: return p,c,score
    p=np.asarray(p,float).copy(); s=p.sum(); if s<=0 return
    p/=s; c=np.convolve(p,p); score=float(2.0*n*np.max(c))
    best_p=p.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); best_score=score
    for _ in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30: break
       M=float(np.max(c))
       if M <= 0: break
       i_idx = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
       j_idx = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
       k_idx = i_idx + j_idx
       vals = np.tile(p, n)
       K = sparse.csr_matrix((vals, (k_idx, i_idx)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
       b_ub = np.full(2*n-1, M)
       c_obj = -np.ones(n)
       try:
          res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=K, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
       except Exception:
          break
       if res is None or not getattr(res,'success',False): break
       g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
       np.clip(g,0,None,out=g)
       gs = float(g.sum())
       if gs <= 1.0: break
       g /= gs
       cg = np.convolve(g,g)
       cross = np.convolve(p,g)
       # choose t grid
       ts = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,41)
       best_t=0; best_val=score
       for t in ts:
          a = float(1.0-t); btt=float(t)
          ct = a*a*c + 2.0*a*btt*cross + btt*btt*cg
          val = float(2.0*n*np.max(ct))
          if val < best_val:
             best_val=val; best_t=t
       if best_val >= score - 1e-10:
          break
       # refine grid around best_t
       lo = max(0.0, best_t-0.2); hi = min(1.0, best_t+0.2)
       if hi > lo:
          ts2 = np.linspace(lo,hi,41)
          for t in ts2:
             a=1-t; btt=t
             ct=...
             val=...
             if val<best_val: ...
       if best_val >= score - 1e-10: break
       p = (1.0-best_t)*p + best_t*g
       s=p.sum()
       if s <=0: break
       p/=s
       c=np.convolve(p,p)
       score=float(2.0*n*np.max(c))
       if score < best_score:
          best_score=score; best_p=p.copy(); best_c=c.copy()
    return best_p,best_c,best_score
```
`linprog` first arg can be keyword `c=`. Use `linprog(c=c_obj, ...)`.

Now, let's write `_coordinate_optimize_until` fully:

```
def _coordinate_optimize_until(p, c, score, deadline, rng, grid, refine_count, max_perturb=3):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    score = float(score)
    n = p.size
    best_p = p.copy(); best_c = c.copy(); best_score = score
    stagnant = 0
    perts = 0
    noise_scales = [0.5, 0.3, 0.15]
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.3:
        order = rng.permutation(n)
        improved = False
        for i in order:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2:
                break
            p, c, score, imp = _coordinate_update(p, c, score, i, grid, refine_count)
            if imp:
                improved = True
        # exact recompute
        s = float(np.sum(p))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            break
        p = p / s
        c = np.convolve(p, p)
        np.clip(c,0,None,out=c)
        score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(c))
        if score < best_score - 1e-12 * max(1.0,best_score):
            best_score = score; best_p = p.copy(); best_c = c.copy()
        if improved:
            stagnant = 0
        else:
            stagnant += 1
            if score > best_score * 1.25 + 1e-8:
                # reset to best to avoid wandering
                p = best_p.copy(); c = best_c.copy(); score = best_score
            if stagnant >= 2 and perts < max_perturb:
                scale = noise_scales[min(perts, len(noise_scales)-1)]
                p = best_p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, scale, n))
                p = np.clip(p,0.0,None)
                s = float(np.sum(p))
                if s > 0:
                    p = p/s
                    c = np.convolve(p,p)
                    score = float(2.0*n*np.max(c))
                    if score < best_score - 1e-12:
                        best_score=score; best_p=p.copy(); best_c=c.copy()
                perts += 1
                stagnant = 0
            elif stagnant >= 4:
                break
    return best_p, best_c, best_score
```
If improved but current score worse than best? Could happen if we perturbed? improved means coordinate decreased from current, but current may be worse than best. We don't update best unless recomputed current beats best. stagnant reset, so may continue exploring. If it stays worse than best, after many improved cycles no best update but stagnant=0; could wander. We can add if `score > best_score + 0.05` and improved for several cycles, reset. But not necessary; coordinate from perturbed likely improves to some local maybe still worse. To avoid wasting, track `cycles_since_best`. If >2 and score > best_score + 0.01, reset. Add:
```
cycles_since_best = 0
...
if score < best_score: cycles_since_best=0; update
else: cycles_since_best +=1
if cycles_since_best > 3 and score > best_score*1.05: reset to best
```

if cycles_since_best >=4 and score > best_score*1.02:
   p=best_p.copy(); c=best_c.copy(); score=best_score; cycles_since_best=0
```

```
def _make_initial(n, strategy, rng):
    n=int(n)
    if n<=0: return np.ones(1)
    if n==1: return np.ones(1)
    st=int(strategy)%12
    if st==0: p=np.ones(n)
    elif st==1: p=rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,1.0))
    elif st==2: p=rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,0.5))
    elif st==3: p=rng.dirichlet(np.full(n,0.2))
    elif st==4: p=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n))
    elif st==5: p=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.4,n))
    elif st==6: p=_paired_initial(n,rng,0.5,0.2)
    elif st==7: p=_paired_initial(n,rng,1.0,1.0)
    elif st==8: p=_two_level(n)
    elif st==9: x=np.linspace(0,1,n); p=np.exp(x)*(1+0.2*rng.random(n))
    elif st==10: x=np.linspace(0,1,n); p=np.exp(-x)*(1+0.2*rng.random(n))
    else: p=(rng.random(n) > 0.5).astype(np.float64); p += 0.05*rng.random(n)
    p=_clean_array(p); s=p.sum(); if s<=0: p=np.ones(n); else p/=s
    return p
```
Strategy 11 binary. Good.

`_paired_initial` parameters alpha_w, alpha_split. For n large, loop in Python may be a bit slow but okay (3809/2 ~1900). For many small, fine. Could vectorize but okay.

Now, `_two_level`:
```
def _two_level(n):
   if n<=1: return np.ones(n)
   p=np.zeros(n)
   m=n//2
   if m>0: p[:m] = (2.0/3.0)/m
   rest=n-m
   if rest>0: p[m:] = (1.0/3.0)/rest
   s=p.sum(); if s>0: p/=s
   return p
```
For n=3809, first1904 mass2/3, rest1905 mass1/3. Score close 16/9? Slight asymmetry maybe okay.

    if n <= 16: return 70,25
    if n <= 32: return 60,22
    if n <= 64: return 50,18
    if n <= 128: return 40,14
    if n <= 256: return 32,10

Now, `_expand_to_length` with prototype length maybe larger than target? If R > target_len, can't have positive blocks. But our proto lengths <=256 <3809. For best_seq maybe length 5000? We only expand small candidates. If target_len <=0 no.

```
def _expand_to_length(proto, target_len):
   target_len=int(target_len)
   p=_clean_array(proto)
   if p.size==0 or p.sum()<=0: return np.ones(target_len)
   p/=p.sum()
   R=p.size
   if R >= target_len:
       # if R==target_len return p; if larger, take first target_len? 
       if R == target_len: return p[:target_len].copy()
       # downsample by floor blocks? Just average chunks of ceil R/target
       out = np.ones(target_len)
       for j in range(target_len):
           start = j*R//target_len; end=max(start+1,(j+1)*R//target_len)
           out[j] = p[start:end].mean()
       out/=out.sum(); return out
   base = target_len // R
   rem = target_len % R
   lengths = np.full(R, base, dtype=int)
   if rem: lengths[:rem] +=1
   out=np.empty(target_len)
   idx=0
   for i in range(R):
       L=int(lengths[i])
       if L>0:
           out[idx:idx+L] = p[i]/L
           idx += L
   # if due to zeros? lengths positive because target_len>=R and base>=1
   s=out.sum(); if s>0: out/=s
   return out
```
For R=256,target=3809 base14 rem151, lengths 15/14. Good.

If target_len < R, the downsample by averaging may reduce score? But not used.

```
def _choose_return(best_seq,best_score,best_full_seq,best_full_score,target):
   if best_full_seq is not None and best_full_score < np.inf:
       if best_full_score <= target + 0.02 or best_full_score <= best_score + 0.02:
           return best_full_seq
   if best_seq is not None:
       return best_seq
   return np.ones(3809)
```

```python
"""
Heuristic minimizer for 2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a))^2 with a >= 0.

Strategy:
  * Optimize short "prototype" sequences by coordinate-wise line search.
  * The coordinate objective is exactly minimized in 1-D subspaces by dense
    logarithmic grids plus local refinement.
  * Multiple random starts are used, including Dirichlet, exponential,
    mirror-paired anti-correlated starts, binary-noise starts, and two-level
    half blocks.
  * Good prototypes are expanded to length 3809 by replacing each coordinate
    with a constant block, which asymptotically preserves the prototype score.
  * The expanded length-3809 sequence is then polished by the same coordinate
    descent on the full length.
  * As a nonlinear escape device, selected short sequences are optionally
    polished with a small LP direction inspired by the provided state-of-art.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _clean_array(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0, out=a)
    return a


def _normalize(a):
    a = _clean_array(a)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(a.size if a.size > 0 else 1, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def _score_normalized(p, c):
    n = int(p.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * max(0.0, mx))


def _score_array(p):
    p = _normalize(p)
    c = np.convolve(p, p)
    return _score_normalized(p, c)


def _eval_external(seq):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq]))
    except Exception:
        return _score_array(seq)


def _candidate_key(cand):
    return cand[0]


def _add_candidate(candidates, seq, score, limit=10):
    if seq is None or not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    arr = _normalize(seq)
    arr = arr.copy()
    candidates.append([float(score), int(arr.size), arr])
    candidates.sort(key=_candidate_key)
    while len(candidates) > limit:
        candidates.pop()


def _two_level(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    m = n // 2
    if m > 0:
        p[:m] = (2.0 / 3.0) / float(m)
    rest = n - m
    if rest > 0:
        p[m:] = (1.0 / 3.0) / float(rest)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s > 0.0:
        p /= s
    else:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return p


def _paired_initial(n, rng, alpha_w=1.0, alpha_split=0.3):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    m = (n + 1) // 2
    weights = rng.dirichlet(np.full(m, float(alpha_w), dtype=np.float64))
    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    split_alpha = max(0.01, float(alpha_split))
    for r in range(m):
        a = r
        b = n - 1 - r
        if a == b:
            p[a] = weights[r]
        else:
            if rng.random() < 0.72:
                if rng.random() < 0.5:
                    q0, q1 = 1.0, 0.0
                else:
                    q0, q1 = 0.0, 1.0
            else:
                q = rng.dirichlet(np.full(2, split_alpha, dtype=np.float64))
                q0, q1 = float(q[0]), float(q[1])
            p[a] = weights[r] * q0
            p[b] = weights[r] * q1
    return _normalize(p)


def _make_initial(n, strategy, rng):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    st = int(strategy) % 12
    if st == 0:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    elif st == 1:
        p = rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, 1.0, dtype=np.float64))
    elif st == 2:
        p = rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, 0.5, dtype=np.float64))
    elif st == 3:
        p = rng.dirichlet(np.full(n, 0.2, dtype=np.float64))
    elif st == 4:
        p = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n))
    elif st == 5:
        p = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.4, n))
    elif st == 6:
        p = _paired_initial(n, rng, 0.7, 0.2)
    elif st == 7:
        p = _paired_initial(n, rng, 1.0, 0.8)
    elif st == 8:
        p = _two_level(n)
    elif st == 9:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
        p = np.exp(x) * (1.0 + 0.2 * rng.random(n))
    elif st == 10:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
        p = np.exp(-x) * (1.0 + 0.2 * rng.random(n))
    else:
        p = (rng.random(n) > 0.5).astype(np.float64)
        p += 0.05 * rng.random(n)
    return _normalize(p)


def _expand_to_length(proto, target_len):
    target_len = int(target_len)
    p = _normalize(proto)
    R = int(p.size)
    if R <= 0 or target_len <= 0:
        return np.ones(max(1, target_len), dtype=np.float64)
    if R == target_len:
        return p.copy()
    if R > target_len:
        out = np.ones(target_len, dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(target_len):
            lo = (j * R) // target_len
            hi = max(lo + 1, ((j + 1) * R) // target_len)
            chunk = p[lo:hi]
            out[j] = float(np.mean(chunk)) if chunk.size > 0 else 1.0
        return _normalize(out)
    base = target_len // R
    rem = target_len % R
    lengths = np.full(R, base, dtype=np.int64)
    if rem > 0:
        lengths[:rem] += 1
    out = np.zeros(target_len, dtype=np.float64)
    idx = 0
    for i in range(R):
        L = int(lengths[i])
        if L > 0:
            out[idx:idx + L] = p[i] / float(L)
            idx += L
    return _normalize(out)


def _grid_params(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 16:
        return 70, 25
    if n <= 32:
        return 60, 22
    if n <= 64:
        return 50, 18
    if n <= 128:
        return 40, 14
    if n <= 256:
        return 32, 10
    if n <= 1024:
        return 22, 7
    return 16, 5


def _coordinate_update(p, c, score, i, grid, refine_count):
    n = int(p.size)
    old = float(p[i])
    if not np.isfinite(old) or old < 0.0:
        old = 0.0
    D = 1.0 - old
    if D < 0.0:
        D = 0.0

    ks = slice(i, i + n)
    c_k = c[ks]

    coeff = 2.0 * p.copy()
    const = -2.0 * old * p.copy()
    mid = int(i)
    if 0 <= mid < n:
        coeff[mid] = 0.0
        const[mid] = -old * old

    max_unaff = 0.0
    if i > 0:
        m = float(np.max(c[:i]))
        if np.isfinite(m) and m > max_unaff:
            max_unaff = m
    end = i + n
    if end < 2 * n - 1:
        m = float(np.max(c[end:]))
        if np.isfinite(m) and m > max_unaff:
            max_unaff = m

    xs = np.empty(int(grid.size) + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    xs[:-1] = grid
    xs[-1] = old
    xs = xs[np.isfinite(xs) & (xs >= 0.0) & (xs <= 1.0e4)]
    if xs.size == 0:
        return p, c, score, False

    aff = c_k[None, :] + coeff[None, :] * xs[:, None] + const[None, :]
    aff[:, mid] += xs * xs
    max_aff = np.max(aff, axis=1)
    maxc = np.maximum(max_unaff, max_aff)
    S = D + xs
    vals = np.full(xs.size, float("inf"), dtype=np.float64)
    valid = S > 1.0e-15
    vals[valid] = (2.0 * n) * maxc[valid] / (S[valid] * S[valid])
    idx = int(np.argmin(vals))
    best_x = float(xs[idx])
    best_val = float(vals[idx])
    tol = 1.0e-12 * max(1.0, float(score))
    if not np.isfinite(best_val) or best_val >= score - tol:
        return p, c, score, False

    if refine_count > 0 and xs.size > 1:
        left = 0.0 if idx == 0 else float(xs[idx - 1])
        right = float(xs[idx]) * 10.0 if idx == xs.size - 1 else float(xs[idx + 1])
        if best_x > 0.0:
            local = best_x * np.logspace(-1.0, 1.0, int(refine_count))
        else:
            base_right = right if right > 0.0 else 1.0
            base_left = max(0.0, left)
            if base_right > base_left:
                local = np.logspace(max(-12.0, np.log10(max(base_left, 1.0e-9))),
                                    np.log10(base_right),
                                    int(refine_count))
            else:
                local = np.logspace(-8.0, 0.0, int(refine_count))
        local = np.concatenate((local, [left, best_x, right]))
        local = np.unique(local[np.isfinite(local) & (local >= 0.0) & (local <= 1.0e4)])
        if local.size > 0:
            aff = c_k[None, :] + coeff[None, :] * local[:, None] + const[None, :]
            aff[:, mid] += local * local
            max_aff = np.max(aff, axis=1)
            maxc = np.maximum(max_unaff, max_aff)
            S = D + local
            vals2 = np.full(local.size, float("inf"), dtype=np.float64)
            valid = S > 1.0e-15
            vals2[valid] = (2.0 * n) * maxc[valid] / (S[valid] * S[valid])
            idx2 = int(np.argmin(vals2))
            if float(vals2[idx2]) < best_val:
                best_x = float(local[idx2])
                best_val = float(vals2[idx2])

    if not np.isfinite(best_val) or best_val >= score - tol:
        return p, c, score, False

    S = D + best_x
    if S <= 1.0e-15:
        return p, c, score, False
    S2 = S * S

    c_new = c / S2
    aff_best = c_k + coeff * best_x + const
    aff_best[mid] += best_x * best_x
    c_new[ks] = aff_best / S2

    p_new = p / S
    p_new[i] = best_x / S

    snew = float(np.sum(p_new))
    if not np.isfinite(snew) or snew <= 0.0:
        return p, c, score, False
    p_new = p_new / snew
    c_new = c_new / (snew * snew)

    np.clip(p_new, 0.0, None, out=p_new)
    np.clip(c_new, 0.0, None, out=c_new)

    s2 = float(np.sum(p_new))
    if not np.isfinite(s2) or s2 <= 0.0:
        return p, c, score, False
    p_new = p_new / s2
    c_new = c_new / (s2 * s2)

    score_new = _score_normalized(p_new, c_new)
    if not np.isfinite(score_new) or score_new >= score - tol:
        return p, c, score, False
    return p_new, c_new, score_new, True


def _coordinate_optimize_until(p, c, score, deadline, rng, grid, refine_count, max_perturb=3):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    score = float(score)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        c = np.convolve(p, p)
        score = _score_normalized(p, c)

    n = int(p.size)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_c = c.copy()
    best_score = score

    stagnant = 0
    cycles_since_best = 0
    perts = 0
    noise_scales = [0.55, 0.30, 0.14, 0.06]

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.3:
        order = rng.permutation(n)
        improved = False
        for i in order:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2:
                break
            p, c, score, imp = _coordinate_update(p, c, score, int(i), grid, refine_count)
            if imp:
                improved = True

        s = float(np.sum(p))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            break
        p = p / s
        c = np.convolve(p, p)
        np.clip(c, 0.0, None, out=c)
        score = _score_normalized(p, c)

        tol = 1.0e-12 * max(1.0, best_score)
        if score < best_score - tol:
            best_score = score
            best_p = p.copy()
            best_c = c.copy()
            cycles_since_best = 0
        else:
            cycles_since_best += 1

        if improved:
            stagnant = 0
        else:
            stagnant += 1
            if score > best_score * 1.25 + 1.0e-8:
                p = best_p.copy()
                c = best_c.copy()
                score = best_score

        if cycles_since_best >= 4 and score > best_score * 1.02:
            p = best_p.copy()
            c = best_c.copy()
            score = best_score
            cycles_since_best = 0

        if stagnant >= 2 and perts < max_perturb:
            scale = noise_scales[min(perts, len(noise_scales) - 1)]
            p = best_p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, scale, n))
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
            s = float(np.sum(p))
            if np.isfinite(s) and s > 0.0:
                p = p / s
                c = np.convolve(p, p)
                np.clip(c, 0.0, None, out=c)
                score = _score_normalized(p, c)
                if score < best_score - 1.0e-10:
                    best_score = score
                    best_p = p.copy()
                    best_c = c.copy()
                    cycles_since_best = 0
            perts += 1
            stagnant = 0
        elif stagnant >= 6:
            break

    return best_p, best_c, best_score


def _lp_polish(p, c, score, deadline, max_iters=2, max_n=96):
    try:
        from scipy.optimize import linprog
        from scipy import sparse
    except Exception:
        return p, c, score

    n = int(p.size)
    if n < 2 or n > int(max_n):
        return p, c, score

    p = _normalize(p)
    c = np.convolve(p, p)
    score = _score_normalized(p, c)

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_c = c.copy()
    best_score = score

    for _ in range(int(max_iters)):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30.0:
            break
        M = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break

        i_idx = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
        j_idx = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
        k_idx = i_idx + j_idx
        vals = np.tile(p, n)
        K = sparse.csr_matrix((vals, (k_idx, i_idx)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n))
        b_ub = np.full(2 * n - 1, M, dtype=np.float64)
        obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

        try:
            res = linprog(
                c=obj,
                A_ub=K,
                b_ub=b_ub,
                bounds=[(0.0, None)] * n,
                method="highs",
                options={"presolve": True}
            )
        except Exception:
            break

        if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
            break
        g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        np.clip(g, 0.0, None, out=g)
        gs = float(np.sum(g))
        if not np.isfinite(gs) or gs <= 1.0:
            break
        g = g / gs

        cg = np.convolve(g, g)
        cross = np.convolve(p, g)

        best_t = 0.0
        best_val = float(score)
        ts = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 33)
        for t in ts:
            a = float(1.0 - t)
            b = float(t)
            ct = a * a * c + 2.0 * a * b * cross + b * b * cg
            val = _score_normalized(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), ct)
            if val < best_val:
                best_val = val
                best_t = float(t)

        if best_val >= float(score) - 1.0e-10:
            break

        lo = max(0.0, best_t - 0.25)
        hi = min(1.0, best_t + 0.25)
        if hi > lo:
            ts2 = np.linspace(lo, hi, 49)
            for t in ts2:
                a = float(1.0 - t)
                b = float(t)
                ct = a * a * c + 2.0 * a * b * cross + b * b * cg
                val = _score_normalized(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), ct)
                if val < best_val:
                    best_val = val
                    best_t = float(t)

        if best_val >= float(score) - 1.0e-10:
            break

        p = (1.0 - best_t) * p + best_t * g
        p = _normalize(p)
        c = np.convolve(p, p)
        np.clip(c, 0.0, None, out=c)
        score = _score_normalized(p, c)
        if score < best_score - 1.0e-12:
            best_score = score
            best_p = p.copy()
            best_c = c.copy()

    return best_p, best_c, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Return the best nonnegative coefficient sequence found within budget_s."""
    budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget - 5.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    target = 1.5027
    final_len = 3809

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    best_full_seq = None
    best_full_score = float("inf")

    def _set_any(seq, sc):
        nonlocal best_seq, best_score
        if seq is None or sc is None:
            return
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc)
            best_seq = _normalize(seq).copy()

    def _set_full(seq, sc):
        nonlocal best_full_seq, best_full_score
        if seq is None or sc is None:
            return
        if seq.size != final_len:
            return
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_full_score:
            best_full_score = float(sc)
            best_full_seq = _normalize(seq).copy()

    # Seed with provided state-of-art if available.
    try:
        hseq = _clean_array(height_sequence_1)
        if hseq.size > 0:
            sc = _score_array(hseq)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                _set_any(hseq, sc)
                if hseq.size == final_len:
                    _set_full(hseq, sc)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # A guaranteed length-3809 baseline: two-level half block (score about 16/9).
    try:
        base_full = _two_level(final_len)
        sc = _score_array(base_full)
        _set_any(base_full, sc)
        _set_full(base_full, sc)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # If time is tiny, return current best.
    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 15.0:
        if best_seq is None:
            best_seq = _two_level(max(1, final_len))
        return [float(x) for x in best_seq]

    reserve_full = max(320.0, 0.35 * budget)
    proto_deadline = deadline - reserve_full
    if proto_deadline < time.monotonic() + 30.0:
        proto_deadline = deadline - 20.0
    if proto_deadline < time.monotonic() + 20.0:
        proto_deadline = deadline - 10.0

    proto_candidates = []

    plan = [
        (8, 10, 3.0),
        (12, 10, 4.0),
        (16, 10, 5.0),
        (24, 8, 6.0),
        (32, 6, 8.0),
        (48, 4, 10.0),
        (64, 4, 12.0),
        (96, 3, 18.0),
        (128, 3, 25.0),
        (160, 3, 35.0),
        (192, 2, 45.0),
        (256, 2, 60.0),
        (384, 2, 80.0),
        (512, 2, 100.0),
    ]

    for n, trials, t_slice in plan:
        if time.monotonic() >= proto_deadline:
            break
        if best_score < target + 0.005:
            break

        grid_count, refine_count = _grid_params(n)
        grid = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.logspace(-8.0, 2.0, grid_count)))

        for trial in range(int(trials)):
            if time.monotonic() >= proto_deadline:
                break
            trial_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + float(t_slice) + 1.0)
            strategy = int(rng.integers(0, 12))
            p = _make_initial(n, strategy, rng)
            c = np.convolve(p, p)
            sc = _score_normalized(p, c)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                _set_any(p, sc)
                _add_candidate(proto_candidates, p, sc)

            if time.monotonic() < trial_deadline - 1.0:
                p, c, sc = _coordinate_optimize_until(
                    p, c, sc, trial_deadline, rng, grid, refine_count, max_perturb=3
                )
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    _set_any(p, sc)
                    _add_candidate(proto_candidates, p, sc)

    # Optional LP polish on promising short prototypes.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - reserve_full - 30.0 and len(proto_candidates) > 0:
        lp_deadline = min(deadline - reserve_full - 10.0, time.monotonic() + 120.0)
        proto_candidates.sort(key=_candidate_key)
        for cand in proto_candidates[:5]:
            if time.monotonic() >= lp_deadline:
                break
            sc, nproto, pproto = cand
            if nproto > 96:
                continue
            if not np.isfinite(sc):
                continue
            cproto = np.convolve(pproto, pproto)
            p2, c2, sc2 = _lp_polish(pproto, cproto, sc, lp_deadline, max_iters=2, max_n=96)
            if np.isfinite(sc2):
                _set_any(p2, sc2)
                _add_candidate(proto_candidates, p2, sc2)

    # Generate / evaluate length-3809 starts before final polishing.
    init_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 90.0)
    full_strategies = [8, 6, 7, 3, 2, 1, 4, 5, 11]
    for st in full_strategies:
        if time.monotonic() >= init_deadline:
            break
        try:
            pfull = _make_initial(final_len, st, rng)
            cfull = np.convolve(pfull, pfull)
            scfull = _score_normalized(pfull, cfull)
            if np.isfinite(scfull):
                _set_any(pfull, scfull)
                _set_full(pfull, scfull)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Expand best prototypes to length 3809.
    if len(proto_candidates) > 0:
        proto_candidates.sort(key=_candidate_key)
        expand_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 120.0)
        for cand in proto_candidates[:8]:
            if time.monotonic() >= expand_deadline:
                break
            sc, nproto, pproto = cand
            if not np.isfinite(sc):
                continue
            try:
                pexp = _expand_to_length(pproto, final_len)
                cexp = np.convolve(pexp, pexp)
                scexp = _score_normalized(pexp, cexp)
                if np.isfinite(scexp):
                    _set_any(pexp, scexp)
                    _set_full(pexp, scexp)
            except Exception:
                pass

    # If no length-3809 sequence exists, create one from the best short candidate.
    if best_full_seq is None:
        if best_seq is not None:
            best_full_seq = _expand_to_length(best_seq, final_len)
            best_full_score = _score_array(best_full_seq)
        else:
            best_full_seq = _two_level(final_len)
            best_full_score = _score_array(best_full_seq)

    # Final coordinate polish on the best length-3809 sequence.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 25.0:
        full_grid_count, full_refine_count = _grid_params(final_len)
        full_grid = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.logspace(-8.0, 2.0, full_grid_count)))
        p = best_full_seq.copy()
        c = np.convolve(p, p)
        sc = _score_normalized(p, c)
        try:
            p, c, sc = _coordinate_optimize_until(
                p, c, sc,
                deadline - 15.0,
                rng,
                full_grid,
                full_refine_count,
                max_perturb=2
            )
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                _set_any(p, sc)
                _set_full(p, sc)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Choose final sequence. Prefer the authoritative evaluator on final candidates.
    final_candidates = []
    if best_full_seq is not None:
        final_candidates.append(best_full_seq)
    if best_seq is not None:
        final_candidates.append(best_seq)
    if not final_candidates:
        final_candidates.append(_two_level(final_len))

    chosen = final_candidates[0]
    chosen_score = _eval_external(chosen)
    for seq in final_candidates[1:]:
        if seq is chosen:
            continue
        sc = _eval_external(seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < chosen_score:
            chosen = seq
            chosen_score = sc

    # As a strong tie-breaker, if the 3809 sequence is nearly as good, return it.
    if best_full_seq is not None:
        sc_full = _eval_external(best_full_seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc_full) and sc_full <= chosen_score + 0.02:
            chosen = best_full_seq
            chosen_score = sc_full

    out = _normalize(chosen)
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
